Concord · Portfolio Command

Exporting or reopening this snapshot does not run a new simulation.

Limitations

  • Live computation on explicitly synthetic operational inputs; registry records supply identifiers, titles and sponsors only. No live Regeneron relationship or actual recruitment progress.
  • Current registry captures are retrospective context, never historical as-of features. No clinical outcomes, historical backtest or acceleration evidence is inferred.
  • Independent mean-one lognormal edge-rate shocks and continuous enrollment are assumptions. P10–P90 ranges and deadline draw fractions are not calibrated predictive probabilities.
  • Small admitted instances exhaustively enumerate all usable networks on the supplied finite draw bank. Larger instances use bounded beam search with unknown optimality gap. Status and termination are reported per strategy; this is not a clinical optimum. No-loser constrains expected trial times.
  • Site capacity is shared proportionally between activated unfinished trials. Static concurrent-trial slots are reserved for the whole network; outside trials and eligibility overlap are unknown.
  • Budget feasibility reserves operating/monitoring costs for the full horizon and maximum selected per-participant cost for all remaining participants. Budget assurance is limited to the modeled cost scope and horizon.
  • Unfinished draws are censored at the horizon. Their capped durations are lower bounds; no complete-time estimate is claimed and such networks are ineligible for recommendation.
  • Both Regeneron-context trials use the NCT05800015 illustrative authored financial schedule; it is an explicit proxy for NCT05785767, not its actual protocol.
  • Potential payer costs, participant cost sharing and reserves reuse Financial Coordination. Coverage is fixed across scenarios and never optimized; no reduction of care or cost shifting is used.
  • Additional support does not change rates unless an explicit hypothetical effect is supplied. Administrative reductions release budget only when marked realized.
  • Hospital contribution uses the inherited 80% operating-resource and 70% procedure-resource assumptions. A nonnegative contribution is an economic scenario constraint, not a fair-market-value or legal determination.
  • Financial Coordination is a sponsor-budget worksheet with no time model. Only Portfolio Command simulates assumed enrollment timing; payer amounts never cause a sponsor saving or rate effect.
  • Site participant/screening costs must exclude every itemized coverage procedure to avoid duplicate charges. Access support is travel/lodging and is not netted against cost sharing.
  • Synthetic financial demonstration: all candidate sites, capacities, costs, margins and payer shares are hypothetical, not registered site facts or negotiated prices.
  • The real trial identifier links an existing illustrative authored schedule; the registry does not publish the complete protocol. This is not a sponsor-approved protocol budget.
  • Medicare and insurer amounts for routine care are benchmarks: they inform fair-market-value reasoning and are inputs to the sponsor's budget negotiation with each site or hospital.
  • Whether an item may be billed to an insurer follows the protocol and coverage rules (for Medicare, NCD 310.1 routine costs in qualifying trials): research-only items are the sponsor's and are never billed to an insurer or participant. The site contract can still have the sponsor pay for a routine item (mark it SPONSOR here), and an item the sponsor pays for or supplies free is then not billed to the insurer. Insurer payment amounts are not a lever over who is billed.
  • In this worksheet, payer type, payer share and the commercial-rate assumption change only the insurer and participant lines; the sponsor commitment does not fall because an insurer pays more for routine care. A line moves onto the sponsor through coverage review or a contract term (for example, the site contract has the sponsor pay for a routine item), and off the sponsor only when coverage analysis finds it is billable routine care that the contract and consent form do not promise to provide free; never through the insurer's payment amount. Payer and Medicare amounts can still inform the rate negotiated for research items (benchmark x the sponsor-site negotiated rate).
  • Routine coverage is potential, subject to qualifying-trial, beneficiary, jurisdiction, plan, network and service review. No insurer participates or commits funding.
  • Unknown responsibility is held as sponsor contingency, not billed to insurers or participants. Unresolved patient exposure is unknown, not evidence of zero liability.
  • Only the authored non-repeating visits (screening, cycles 1-8, end of treatment, safety follow-up) are priced. Actual drug acquisition/manufacturing, later treatment, complications and downstream outcomes are unknown; the drug contingency is an assumption, not a complete trial cost.
  • Access support reimburses trial-related travel and lodging; it is not netted against, and does not pay, insurer cost sharing. Whether a sponsor may cover a beneficiary's cost sharing is a separate compliance question this worksheet does not model.
  • Each worksheet line is assigned to one party (sponsor, insurer, participant or sponsor contingency). That is not a billing-compliance review and does not establish that no item is billed twice. Site per-participant costs must exclude every itemized worksheet procedure, or that procedure is counted twice.
  • Participants are split across selected sites in proportion to stated capacity, an operator input; when an existing network that lacks capacity is extended, existing sites are filled to their stated capacity first and only the shortfall goes to added sites. It is not a measured or predicted enrollment.
  • Sensitivity varies only site cost inputs (seeded lognormal spread); the ranges are not calibrated intervals.
  • Sponsor-to-hospital payments are transfers. Consolidated resource costs use separate explicit cost assumptions; a lower sponsor payment is not automatically a system or payer saving.
  • No claims, reimbursements, coverage authorizations, contracts or patient-identifiable records are transmitted. Operator corrections are interpretations, not clinical or payer attestations.
